--- original brief ---
# Codex verification brief — sentence-level claim check (commit 0c441e0)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. You may write scratch
files only under `audit_evidence_claims/`. End with a verdict line, exactly one of:

    VERDICT: APPROVE
    VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED

## What changed

`OncoKG.check_groundedness` (src/query.py) checks a *generated* answer against the
evidence retrieved for the question. Before this commit it only checked that names and
numbers appeared somewhere in the evidence, so "AstraZeneca has discontinued
osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR" and "osimertinib is safe" passed.

Now, given `linker=self.link_entities`, it also calls `claims.check_claims`
(src/claims.py), which reads the answer sentence by sentence:

1. A stated relation (acts on / evaluates / sponsors) must hold between the same
   entities in an evidence statement of that relation.
2. Negations, comparisons, hedges and "develops/owns/markets" are reported unless the
   sentence is quoting an evidence statement about the same entities.
3. "phase N" and "N trials" must be stated for that sentence's own entities (a named
   trial is the most specific subject).
4. Every other content word must be grammar (FUNCTION list) or appear in an evidence
   statement about the sentence's entities.

Also: `link_entities` no longer joins several short words into a one-word drug
abbreviation ("is a" was read as "isa" = isatuximab).

The goal set by the owner: ordinary-word claims ("safe", "discontinued") and relations
must be caught. The design is deliberately strict: a true fact the graph does not hold
is reported as unverifiable, and a human reviewer decides.

## What to verify (reproduce everything; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **False negatives (most important).** Find answers containing a claim the retrieved
   evidence does not support that still return `grounded=True`. Use the real graph:
   `python src/app.py` exposes `Engine().check(question, answer)`; or call
   `OncoKG().check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, True, vocab, linker=kg.link_entities)`.
   Try at least: reversed relations, wrong drug–target / company–drug / trial–drug
   pairing in multi-entity sentences, pronouns ("it", "this drug"), relations stated
   with nouns ("an EGFR inhibitor", "AstraZeneca's osimertinib"), status/phase of the
   wrong trial, counts, negation spelled differently ("isn't", "no longer",
   "un-", "non-"), clinical claims made only of words that happen to be in evidence
   statements, claims split across sentences, lists, Korean or mixed text, markdown.
B. **False positives.** Faithful answers an LLM would plausibly write from the evidence
   (paraphrases, "is an inhibitor of", "is being tested in", lists) that are flagged.
   Strict is intended, but a check that flags most faithful answers is not useful —
   say how often it happens on realistic paraphrases of each demo question in
   `src/query.py:main`.
C. **Correctness of the code**: regexes, sentence splitting, the subject-inheritance
   rule for fragments of one line, `quoted()` bigram rule (can it be abused to launder a
   negation?), dedup in `check_groundedness` (one finding per value — can it hide a
   finding?), the `link_entities` change (does it break any multi-word drug name the
   pipeline should still link? check against the dictionary).
D. **Regressions**: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (79 expected), and
   that the template answers for the demo questions, read as prose, still pass.
E. **Documentation honesty**: README.md / README.ko.md item 13, the docstrings in
   claims.py and query.py, web/template.html section 06, web/try.html box 3 — do they
   claim more than the code does?

## Report format

`BLOCKERS` (claims that pass and should not, or wrong code), `SHOULD FIX`, `NOTES`,
each with a reproducible input and the observed output. Then the verdict line.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent claim-check audit, round 5

Reviewed HEAD **9fda6f8ec36e8b49d75e2d51d7f73ad3ba21e359**, diff `git diff 9fda6f8^...HEAD`, on the real local graph. **The exact earlier attacks are repaired, but new unsupported claims still pass outside README item 13's explicit residue.** No tracked files were edited. All new scratch artifacts are under `audit_evidence_claims/r5/`; pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were not edited by the review.

Support means support in the question's actual retrieved evidence, not an external medical judgment. New blockers below use `Engine().check(question, answer)` with unmodified retrievals, not synthetic evidence. A passing evidence-consistent answer is a NOTE. The disclosed one-statement rearrangement residue and artificial encodings are treated separately.

Question keys denote these exact inputs:

| Key | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |

Primary independent confirmations: [root5_confirmation.py](root5_confirmation.py), [root5_confirmation.json](root5_confirmation.json). Exploratory numeric cases: [root5_numeric.py](root5_numeric.py), [root5_numeric.json](root5_numeric.json). Each result preserves the exact input, full check output and complete retrieved evidence, beyond the app's eight-row preview.

## BLOCKERS

Unless a row is explicitly a control, **every answer below returns `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`**.

### B1. Heading substitution still deletes kind and disease claims

All with Q3:

| Exact answer (`\n` is a newline) | Observed |
|---|---|
| `The following companies inhibit EGFR:\n- Osimertinib` | True; [] |
| `These drugs:\n- EGFR` | True; [] |
| `These NSCLC drugs:\n- Osimertinib` | True; [] |
| Control: `The following safe drugs:\n- Osimertinib` | False; `wording: safe` |
| Control: `Osimertinib is an NSCLC drug.` | False; `indication: lung cancer` |
| Faithful control: `The following drugs inhibit EGFR:\n- Osimertinib` | True; [] |

The only Q3 evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. It does not call osimertinib a company, EGFR a drug, or osimertinib an NSCLC drug. These ordinary headings add kind/disease words absent from that statement, so they are outside the accepted one-statement word-rearrangement residue.

**Cause:** `src/claims.py:260-264` replaces the entire determiner-plus-plain-noun slot, including optional `NSCLC`. Adjectives now survive, but a plain noun can itself be false. The first attack becomes `Osimertinib inhibit EGFR`; the kind validator never receives `companies`. Without the slot, `Companies inhibiting EGFR:\n- Osimertinib` rejects both `entity: Companies` and `wording: companies`.

Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json` IDs `B1_*`; [spec5_extended.json](spec5_extended.json), `heading_wrong_kind_drug` and other `heading_*` cases. The exact R4 safe/discontinued heading repairs remain FIXED.

### B2. Named groups borrow the first member's clinical-trial context

Q4:

```text
LORLATINIB and PLB1003 are in clinical trials.
```

True; []. The more explicit `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 have clinical trials in this graph.` also passes, as does `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 inhibit ALK in clinical trials.`

Retrieved evidence:

```text
LORLATINIB acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage APPROVAL; 13 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
```

No Q4 statement contains `clinical`. PLB1003's row explicitly gives zero trials in this graph. The shared clinical-trial assertion therefore borrows another drug's positive context; it is not merely the supported assertion that both inhibit ALK.

**Cause:** `_check_attributes` picks `subjects[0]` at `src/claims.py:771`; `wide` is now scoped only to that first subject at `:802`. `about_trials` then licenses all clinical/trial words at `:808-811,1013-1018`. Explicit named subject groups are not expanded by the per-member paths at `:383-398`, which handle pronouns and elision.

Controls: reverse the names to `PLB1003 and LORLATINIB are in clinical trials.` -> False, `wording: clinical`, `wording: trials`; singular PLB1003 -> the same findings. `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 inhibit ALK. They are in clinical trials.` also rejects. Supported LORLATINIB + CRIZOTINIB clinical context passes. This order dependence isolates the first-member leak, outside the one-statement residue.

Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B2_*`; [spec5_controls.json](spec5_controls.json), `clinical_group_graph` and reverse; [spec5_findings.md](spec5_findings.md).

### B3. `It` after a passive list still misses the documented group check

Q4:

```text
ALK is targeted by LORLATINIB and PLB1003. It is approved.
```

True; []. Changing `It` to `They` rejects `wording: approved`. Using the active first sentence, `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 target ALK. It is approved.`, also rejects. The faithful passive LORLATINIB + CRIZOTINIB list followed by `They are approved.` passes.

**Cause:** the passive sentence leaves `st.group` as `[ALK]`. The largest same-type group from `st.last` is selected at `src/claims.py:368-369` only when the pronoun belongs to `PLURAL`; `it` does not (`:155`). It resolves to ALK at `:377-379`, whose evidence pool contains other drugs' approval. This is the opposite of the explicit B2 repair claim in `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:10` and the per-member `it` description in `claims.py:32-33`.

Approval of the target ALK is unsupported, and the documented conservative interpretation of the two-drug list must also reject because PLB1003 has only PHASE_1. Because the pronoun can be semantically ambiguous and the later clause can resemble the broad single-row residue, this is principally a **confirmed repair/code/documentation discrepancy**, not the sole basis for the verdict. B1, B2, B4, B5 and B6 independently fail the outside-residue bar.

Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B3_*`; `spec5_probe.json`, `passive_it_approval` / `passive_they_approval`.

### B4. Parenthetical metadata breaks shared trial phase/status ownership

Q2, both pass:

```text
NCT03909334 (phase 2) and NCT07680790 (phase 3) are recruiting.
```

```text
NCT03909334 (active, not recruiting) and NCT07680790 are phase 3.
```

Evidence:

```text
NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
```

The first answer incorrectly calls NCT03909334 recruiting; the second assigns it phase 3. Each uses a natural parenthetical descriptor that is itself correct.

**Cause:** `trial_run` at `src/claims.py:814-826` asks `_group` for a contiguous run around the nearest trial. The intervening phase/status tokens stop the run before the earlier trial (`_group`, `:561-580`). Shared phase validation at `:849-860` or shared status validation at `:935-947` therefore checks only the last trial. The named subjects still include both trials; no general rule requires their shared attributes to cover the full subject list.

Controls: removing the parentheticals makes both claims fail (`status: recruiting` / `phase: phase 3`). Reversing the first answer's trial order makes it fail `status: recruiting`. The faithful `NCT07680790 and NCT02411448 are phase 3.` passes. This transfers values across two records, outside the explicit one-statement residue.

Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B4_*`; `root5_numeric.json`, `status_phase_parentheses`, `status_phase_one`, `phase_context_parentheses`, and controls.

### B5. Counts escape through longer labels and cross-sentence references

Q5:

```text
AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in trials in this graph: 10.
```

True; []. The evidence has **14** trials for OSIMERTINIB and **10** for CARBOPLATIN; it does not establish a shared ten-trial count. The exact R4 immediate label `... in trials: 10.` now rejects `count_ambiguous`, but ordinary inserted words bypass it.

**Cause:** trial-count detection only examines three surrounding tokens (`src/claims.py:878-881`), while shared-count detection examines three after/two before (`:905-907`). `in this graph:` pushes `trials` out of both windows. The number is treated as an untyped figure and validated against the nearest drug, CARBOPLATIN, using `scoped(..., "after")` (`:777-789,882-889`). `... in trials recorded: 10.` also passes. Correct separate `14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin` passes; swapped counts reject both numbers.

A more natural cross-sentence variant also passes:

```text
AstraZeneca sponsors trials of Tagrisso. There are 10 such trials in this graph.
```

The faithful 14 version passes too. Replacing the second sentence with `This drug has 10 such trials in this graph.` rejects number 10. `AstraZeneca sponsors trials of Tagrisso and has 10 such trials.` also passes. `such` refers back to osimertinib trials, but the state retains AstraZeneca as the subject (`:470-471,281-288`), the elided-subject rule reuses it (`:427-433`), and numeric scope then needs only the sponsor, allowing CARBOPLATIN's row. There is no retained sponsor-drug pair for `such trials`. This is value transfer across two statements, not a harmless rewording of one record.

Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B5_*`; `root5_numeric.json`, `count_label_*`, `count_such_*`, `count_separate_*`; independent confirmation in [spec5_anaphora.json](spec5_anaphora.json) and [spec5_anaphora_findings.md](spec5_anaphora_findings.md).

### B6. Kind words still accept the wrong owner or confuse participation with identity

| Q | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `EGFR, which is inhibited by osimertinib, is a drug.` | True; [] |
| Q3 | `The drug EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib.` | True; [] |
| Q2 | `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` | True; [] |

The first two call the target EGFR a drug; the third calls a drug a trial. The Q3 evidence is only `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. Q2 evidence consists of named NCT trials evaluating OSIMERTINIB. None supports these identities. `drug` in the Q3 assertion and `clinical trial` in Q2 are absent from the respective evidence statements, so these are outside the explicit one-statement vocabulary residue.

Three concrete paths:

- At `src/claims.py:987-988`, a kind word binds to the last entity mentioned anywhere before it. A relative clause can put OSIMERTINIB nearer than the main subject EGFR.
- With no preceding entity, the same line accepts `kind in present`, letting the Drug at the other end of the supported relation license `The drug EGFR`.
- `trial`, `trials`, and `clinical` skip that owner test (`:983-985`) and use `about_trials` (`:808-811,1013-1018`). The checker treats `is a trial` like `is in trials`; identity validation requires a named entity on each side (`:443-449`), so it does not intervene.

Controls: R4 `EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.` rejects `wording: drug`; Q3 `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` rejects `clinical` and `trial`; faithful Q2 `Osimertinib is in clinical trials.` and `NCT07680790 is a clinical trial.` pass. The same owner rule also rejects faithful `EGFR is inhibited by the drug osimertinib.` and `Osimertinib, which inhibits EGFR, is a drug.` with `wording: drug`.

Artifacts: `root5_confirmation.json`, `B6_*`, `S1_*`; [docs5_controls_results.json](docs5_controls_results.json), `kind_prefix_*`, `trial_identity*`; [docs5_findings.md](docs5_findings.md).

|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Q1 | 6/6 | 5/6 | 4/8 |
| Q2 | 6/6 | 6/6 | 7/8 |

| Evidence answers | **29/30 (96.7%)** | **29/30 (96.7%)** | **28/40 (70%)** |
| Q6 refusals, separate | 4/6 | 5/6 | 5/6 |

The tested evidence answers pass a majority, so this audit does not claim that most faithful answers fail. All five specifically cited R4 false positives now pass. Fresh examples still rejected:

| Q | Exact faithful answer | Observed (`grounded=False`) |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `EGFR is inhibited by the drug osimertinib.` | `wording: drug` |
| Q1 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 trials of osimertinib. Bristol Myers Squibb sponsors 2 trials of it.` | `number: 2`; `relation_direction: Bristol Myers Squibb sponsors Astrazeneca` |
| Q2 | `NCT07680790 is recruiting. NCT03909334 is active but not recruiting.` | `wording: active`, `wording: but`, `negation: not`, `status: recruiting` |
| Q4 | `The graph records 13 NSCLC trials of lorlatinib and 15 NSCLC trials of crizotinib.` | `number: 15` |
| Q5 | `AstraZeneca runs 6 NSCLC trials of pemetrexed and 6 of cisplatin.` | `count_ambiguous: PEMETREXED / CISPLATIN` |

Naming osimertinib instead of `it` in the Q1 example passes; splitting the Q4 counts into two sentences passes; `active, not recruiting` passes in place of `active but not recruiting`. These isolate parser limitations, rather than lack of supporting evidence. Full failures and source-based validation of all 40 new answers are in [docs5_findings.md](docs5_findings.md), [docs5_faithful_evidence_review.json](docs5_faithful_evidence_review.json), and [docs5_faithful_summary.json](docs5_faithful_summary.json).

Unchanged historical failures include `supports trials`, sponsorship-not-development commentary and refusals naming France, which README already discloses. Two additional old R4 diagnostics still reject: Q4 `LORLATINIB and CRIZOTINIB are approved.` (`wording: approved`) and Q2 `NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 evaluate osimertinib. The former is phase 2 and the latter is phase 3.` (`former`/`latter` and phases). These are not new R5 regressions.

### S2. Documentation axis: better caveats, but incomplete explicit residues and repair claims

No separate repository coding-standard violation is asserted. The specification-axis findings are B1-B6; this documentation assessment is separate.

The earlier blanket criticism should not be repeated: `README.md:508-531`, `README.ko.md:354-366`, `claims.py:10-11,48-51`, `query.py:450-468`, `web/template.html:263-267`, and `web/try.html:69-72,142-145` acknowledge pattern limitations and distinguish no findings from proof of truth. The page now correctly says four rounds. Evidence-validated citation stripping in the global name check behaved as documented in the new controls.

However, the explicit known-pass list (`README.md:520-524`, `README.ko.md:361-363`) does not disclose the heading kind/disease erasure, named-group clinical borrowing, cross-record trial/status/count transfer, or new false kind assertions above. A general unseen-shapes caveat is not disclosure of these now-known residues under this audit's bar.

Concrete precision issues:

- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:10` says `they`/`it` after a passive list stand for both drugs; B3 shows the `it` path does not.
- `claims.py:33-35` broadly describes spelled-out letter rejection and heading preservation. Comma-separated spelling still passes, and the heading's noun/NSCLC modifier can be removed (B1).
- `claims.py:25` and `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:18-19` describe percentage checking/fixing; a space before `%` still defeats it (N2). The guarantee needs this qualification even though this audit conservatively treats the one-row numeric variant as a residue note.
- `claims.py:22-26` describes prior-trial ownership less precisely than `trial_run`, which also handles a following run.
- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:3` says all 438 earlier exact reproductions were rejected, but that denominator included faithful/control cases. It should identify the rejected **attack** cases rather than all 438.

The original exact repairs are genuine; the documentation's problem is the scope claimed for them and omission of newly demonstrated residues.

### N2. Secondary numeric/spelling discrepancies; not independent verdict drivers

Q4 `LORLATINIB has 13 % NSCLC trials.` and its nonbreaking-space version pass; attached `13%` rejects `number: 13%`. `RE_TOKEN` only captures a percent sign attached to digits (`src/claims.py:180-181`); a separate sign is discarded, leaving an ordinary supported count. `root5_numeric.json` records both. Because R4 already treated the one-row percentage transformation as plausibly covered by the broad residue, this review does not promote it to an independent blocker merely because R5 tried to fix it. The blanket percentage-repair statement remains inaccurate.

Q3 `Osimertinib does N, O, T inhibit EGFR.` and `Osimertinib is S, A, F, E.` pass, while whitespace-only letters reject. Commas reset the run at `claims.py:970-979`, and leftover single letters are skipped at `:1020`. These artificial forms are secondary notes about the explicitly stated letter rule, not a basis needed for rejection. Exact outputs are in `docs5_probe_results.json` / `docs5_controls_results.json`.

Evidence-consistent passes are not blockers: supported active/passive inhibition, correct group approval, alias identity, valid citations and correct paired per-drug counts. Q2 `Osimertinib is recruiting.` is conservatively assigned to the disclosed one-statement rearrangement residue, not counted as a new independent blocker.

### Regressions, linker, dedup and filesystem integrity

- `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> **240 passed in 8.01s**, exit 0, empty stderr. TEMP/TMP were confined to `r5/replay5_temp`; bytecode and pytest cache writes were disabled.
- **5/5 evidence template prose bodies pass**, with template headings and the same `Scope:` removal used by the normal path. Scope-inclusive templates and the generated-check France refusal remain rejected; the ordinary template/refusal path is separate. Exact text is in `replay5_faithful_results.json`.
- Dictionary: **7,610 keys / 1,066 drugs / 2,634 unique multiword aliases**. Original short-word-gate replay loses **zero** intended links; `is a` stays unlinked. `AZD 9291`, `osimertinib mesylate`, `trastuzumab deruxtecan` and `pemetrexed disodium` retain their links. Five previously documented dictionary-to-tokenizer discrepancies are unchanged, not new R5 regressions.
- `Osimertinib is safe. EGFR is safe.` preserves two separate contexts. Same-unit `Osimertinib is safe and EGFR is safe.` still merges duplicate `safe` values, but remains False; dedup does not turn a nonempty finding list into a pass.
- ALK question punctuation with `!`, `:` or `;` remains an unchanged linker limitation; `?` works.
- Tracked and staged diffs are empty. HEAD, source/data SHA-256 hashes and fresh confirmation results are in [root5_verification.json](root5_verification.json) and [replay5_verification.json](replay5_verification.json). No source repair, commit, server, external message, network request or model generation was performed.

Reproduce the historical cases and regression suite with `powershell -NoProfile -File audit_evidence_claims/r5/replay5_reproduce.ps1`. Reproduce the new probes with `powershell -NoProfile -File audit_evidence_claims/r5/reproduce5_new.ps1`. Both confine outputs to `r5/`.

reproduced here and was right. Codex's probes were re-run from copies in `audit_evidence_claims/r5_recheck/`.

| # | Finding | Verdict | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B1 | headings: the slot still removed plain nouns ("these drugs" over EGFR, "companies", "NSCLC drugs") | right | only the determiner is replaced; every noun and adjective stays and is checked |
| B2 | a named group borrowed its first member's clinical-trial context | right | trial/NSCLC context must hold for every member of the subject group before the verb |
| B3 | "It" after "ALK is targeted by A and B" resolved to ALK | right | an untyped "it" after a clause naming several entities of one kind stands for all of them, as "they" does; an elided subject after such a passive is checked against ALK's own words, not the drugs' bracketed facts |
| B4 | parentheticals broke shared phase/status ownership | right | a phase or status after the verb is about every trial before the verb |
| B5 | counts escaped through "trials in this graph: 10" and "10 such trials" | right | any earlier trial word with no entity between makes a figure a trial count; a company-only count after a sentence naming one drug is that pair's count (several drugs = count_ambiguous) |
| B6 | kind words bound to the wrong entity; "is a clinical trial" | right | "the drug X" belongs to X; "… is a drug" to the clause's subject; "is a (clinical) trial" requires a trial |
| S1 | 12 fresh faithful forms rejected | right | object "it" after "trials of"; "active but not recruiting"; counts scoped to their own drug; elided "6 of cisplatin" and "8 evaluating gefitinib"; noun relations with the subject listed after "include" or "by"; "as an inhibitor"; "Company: N trials of X" entries; record verbs ("records", "includes") as verbs, with the X-is-Y rule kept to forms of "be" |
| S2 | docs | right | docstring rules and README item 13 updated (five rounds); dashboard text updated |

"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

Three boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, or paste an answer (say, one a language model wrote) and see what the
groundedness check flags against the evidence for that question. All three call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
        return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()

    def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
        """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
        checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
                                       vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
                                       linker=kg.link_entities)
        return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
                "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
                              "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence)}


def make_handler(engine: Engine):
    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
            self.send_response(code)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def do_GET(self):
            if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
                self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            else:
                self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")

        def do_POST(self):
            try:
                n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
                body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
                text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()[:500]
                if not text:
                    raise ValueError("empty input")
                if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check"):
                    return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
                with engine.lock:
                    if self.path == "/api/resolve":
                        res = engine.resolve(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/ask":
                        res = engine.ask(text)
                    else:
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.check(text, answer)
                self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")
            except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
                self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")

        def log_message(self, *a):
            pass
    return Handler


def main() -> None:
    port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
    engine = Engine()
    print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
    ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

  README.md:314:anything shaped like a development code, is emitted as an explicit unresolved
> README.md:315:residue. Shorter leftovers are dropped as parse filler, so a three-letter code
  README.md:316:next to a known drug (`pembrolizumab new XYZ`) disappears without a trace.
  README.md:317:
  README.md:318:**A biosimilar is not its originator.** `Bevacizumab Biosimilar HLX04` and
  README.md:319:`Bevacizumab-Pfizer` name separate regulatory products. Resolving them to
  README.md:320:BEVACIZUMAB puts a competitor's asset on Genentech's molecule and makes the
  README.md:321:asset under study vanish. There is no identifier for these in the vocabulary, so
  README.md:322:they are refused with a reason code. The same rule covers liposomal,
  README.md:323:albumin-bound and nanoparticulate reformulations, biosimilar brands such as
  README.md:324:`Mvasi` and `Zirabev`, and FDA suffixes known to belong to biosimilars
  README.md:325:(`bevacizumab-awwb`) — while an originator's own suffix (`cemiplimab-rwlc`) is
  README.md:326:stripped and resolved. The check runs on the text before cleaning, so a
  README.md:327:reformulation named inside a combination or in brackets is still seen.
  README.md:328:
  README.md:329:**A negated substance is an absence.** `No pembrolizumab` records that the arm
  README.md:330:did *not* receive it, and gets its own tier, `BLOCKED_NEGATED`, rather than an
  README.md:331:`EVALUATES` edge. A prefix is not a negation: `Non-platinum chemotherapy:
  README.md:332:paclitaxel` still resolves paclitaxel.
  README.md:333:
  README.md:507:    when ownership changes.
> README.md:508:13. **The claim check reads word order, not meaning.** Generated answers are read
  README.md:509:    clause by clause into subject, relation and object (`src/claims.py`, rules in
  README.md:510:    its docstring) and compared with the retrieved evidence for those entities. It
  README.md:511:    is a set of patterns, so it is only as good as the sentence shapes it was
  README.md:512:    written for. Five adversarial rounds found 33, about 40, about 25, about 20 and
  README.md:513:    then about 15 shapes that got through (reversed nouns, clefts, a second object after "and
  README.md:514:    on", pronouns read too early, plural pronouns, a count borrowed from a phase or
  README.md:515:    shared by two drugs, `agonist` inside `antagonist`, headings, bracketed text,
  README.md:516:    refused product names, words like "clinical trials" or "drug" treated as
  README.md:517:    grammar, "approved in NSCLC", "the following safe drugs", a phase or status
  README.md:518:    shared by two trials, letters spelled out one by one, a kind word in a heading or
  README.md:519:    a relative clause, "10 such trials"). Each is now a regression
  README.md:520:    test, but a shape
  README.md:521:    nobody has tried yet can still be misread. Known to get through: a clause whose
  README.md:522:    words, entities and figures all come from one evidence statement about those
  README.md:523:    entities but are arranged into a different claim; a drug name outside the
  README.md:524:    vocabulary that does not look like an INN; odd but evidence-consistent wording
  README.md:525:    ("osimertinib is the inhibition of EGFR"). Known to be over-strict: a true fact
  README.md:526:    the graph does not hold, a paraphrase it has no word for ("supports trials"), a

348: def _check_unit(unit, statements, linker, st, flag, lenient=False, joined=False):
349:     toks, ents = _tokenise(unit, linker)
350:     for clause in _clauses(toks):
351:         # pronouns take the subject as it stands at this clause, not at the unit start;
352:         # one after a relation verb is that relation's object ("targets EGFR and
353:         # inhibits it"); a plural one stands for every member of the subject group
354:         plural = None
355:         for k, t in enumerate(clause):
356:             if t[0] != "ENT" or ents[t[1]]["type"] != "Pronoun":
357:                 continue
358:             p = ents[t[1]]
359:             want = p["want"]
360:             back = [x for x in clause[max(0, k - 2):k] if x[0] == "W"]
361:             if want is None and back and back[-1][1] in set(RELATION) | {"on", "of"}:
362:                 verb = next((x[1] for x in reversed(back) if x[1] in RELATION), None)
363:                 if verb:
364:                     want = ROLES[RELATION[verb][0]][1]
365:                 elif back[-1][1] == "of" and len(back) > 1 and back[-2][1] in TRIAL_NOUNS:
366:                     want = "Drug"                     # "2 trials of it"
367:             if want is None:
368:                 # "they" (or an "it" that could be either) after a clause naming several
369:                 # entities of one kind stands for all of them: the subject group, or
370:                 # else the largest set of one kind ("ALK is targeted by A and B")
371:                 kinds_last: dict[str, list] = {}
372:                 for e in st.last:
373:                     kinds_last.setdefault(e.get("type"), []).append(e)
374:                 biggest = max(kinds_last.values(), key=len, default=[])
375:                 members = (list(st.group) if len(st.group) > 1 else
376:                            biggest if len(biggest) > 1 else [])
377:             else:
378:                 members = ([e for e in st.last if e.get("type") == want]
379:                            if p["label"] in PLURAL else [])
380:             if len(members) > 1:

382:                 ents[t[1]] = dict(members[0])

385:             ents[t[1]] = (dict(ante, _pron=True) if ante

389:         elided = pred < len(clause) and not any(t[0] == "ENT" for t in clause[:pred])
390:         if plural:

392:             for m in members:                 # "they are approved": each of them

398:                 names = {m["label"] for m in members}

    nobody has tried yet can still be misread. Known to get through: a clause whose
    words, entities and figures all come from one evidence statement about those
    entities but are arranged into a different claim; a drug name outside the
    vocabulary that does not look like an INN; odd but evidence-consistent wording
    ("osimertinib is the inhibition of EGFR"). Known to be over-strict: a true fact
    the graph does not hold, a paraphrase it has no word for ("supports trials"), a
    sentence about the evidence itself that uses a negation or "develops", and a
    refusal naming something outside the graph ("the capital of France"). Three
    narrow exceptions are deliberate: a line copied from an evidence statement
    passes (its heading is still checked), a clause with no entity that only talks
    about the graph passes, and a bracket is dropped as a citation only when every
    id in it is a record of the retrieved evidence.
14. **Provenance does not record the code version.** Snapshots carry their
    fetch time, but the graph does not store which revision of the rules built
    it, so a change that arrives with a new snapshot and a code change together
    cannot be separated from the database alone; only git history can.
8. **The query layer answers the nearest question it can.** "Is pembrolizumab
   safe in patients with autoimmune disease?" returns a drug profile rather than
   refusing. It refuses only when no entity links at all.

560: def _clauses(toks):
561:     """Split at ; and at and/but/while/whereas when both sides have a predicate."""
562:     joins = ("and", "but", "while", "whereas")
563:     cuts = [-1]
564:     for i, t in enumerate(toks):
565:         if t == ("P", ";"):
566:             cuts.append(i)
567:         elif t[0] == "W" and t[1] in joins:
568:             left = toks[cuts[-1] + 1:i]
569:             nxt = next((j for j in range(i + 1, len(toks))
570:                         if (toks[j][0] == "W" and toks[j][1] in joins)
571:                         or toks[j] == ("P", ";")), len(toks))
572:             right = toks[i + 1:nxt]
573:             if any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in left) and \
574:                     any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in right):
575:                 cuts.append(i)
576:     cuts.append(len(toks))
577:     return [toks[a + 1:b] for a, b in zip(cuts, cuts[1:]) if toks[a + 1:b]]

580: def _group(clause, k, step, skip=()):
581:     """Positions of the entity run nearest to k in direction `step`."""
582:     i = k + step
583:     while 0 <= i < len(clause) and (clause[i][0] != "ENT" or i in skip):

"""Clause-level claim check for a generated answer.

The name-and-number check in query.py catches invented drugs and invented
figures. It cannot catch a false statement built from true tokens:
"AstraZeneca has discontinued osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR",
"osimertinib is safe for all patients", "EGFR acts on osimertinib". This module
reads each clause the way a reviewer would: who is it about, what does it say
about them, and which retrieved statement says the same about the same entities?

What the check asks of each clause (a description of the rules, not a guarantee;
see the last paragraph):

  * Relations (acts on / inhibits / activates, evaluates, sponsors) are read by
    position: the entities before the relation word are its subject, the ones after
    its object, reversed for a passive ("is inhibited by") and for "target of".
    Both sides must be named (or be the subject carried over from the clause before),
    must be the right kinds (a drug acts on a target, a trial evaluates a drug, a
    company sponsors trials of a drug), and an evidence statement of that relation
    must name both. Inhibit/activate must match the edge's action type.
  * In a clause that states a relation, every entity it names must be part of a
    relation that was checked.
  * A phase belongs to the trials named just before it (a run joined by "and" shares
    it), else to a drug's maximum stage; a figure belongs to the nearest entity after
    it and must appear in evidence naming that entity and the clause's subject; a
    trial count must appear as a trial count, a percentage as a percentage.
  * A status belongs to the trial named before it. Words naming a kind of thing
    ("drug", "company", "trial") need an entity of that kind or evidence about its
    trials; "NSCLC" may only qualify trials the evidence calls NSCLC trials, never an
    approval; what a named group of subjects is said to be must hold for every member.
    A word naming a kind belongs to the entity right after it ("the drug X"), to the
    clause's subject after "is a", or else to the entity just before it. A trial count in a clause naming several drugs needs a count
    per drug, and a count after "trials" or referring back ("10 such trials") counts
    as a trial count. Two different entities on either side of a form of "be" that is
    no relation ("X is Y") are reported. A status or phase after the verb is about
    every trial in the subject. A plural pronoun, an elided subject after a group, and an
    "it" after a group are checked once for every entity they stand for. Letters
    spelled out one by one are reported. A heading's determiner ("these", "the
    following") is replaced by the item; every noun and adjective after it stays and is
    checked.
  * Every other content word must appear in evidence naming every entity of the
    clause.
  * Negations, comparisons, hedges and modal verbs, and claims of developing,
    owning or marketing are reported; so are distinct products the resolver refuses,
    pronouns with nothing to refer to, and text that is not English.

Exceptions, all narrow: a line that is a copy of a whole evidence statement (case
and punctuation aside) passes as it stands, though any heading above it is still
checked; a clause that names no entity and uses only words about the graph itself
("this graph cannot answer that question") passes; a bracket is dropped as a
citation only when every id in it is a record of the retrieved evidence.

It is strict on purpose: a true fact the graph does not hold is reported as
unverifiable, and a reviewer decides. It reads words and their order, not meaning,
so a sentence it cannot parse into the shapes above can be misread; the known
residue is listed in the README (known defect 13).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

# -- vocabulary --------------------------------------------------------------- #
# relation word -> (relation, required action, voice). Required action: None means
# any edge of that relation; "inhib" / "activ" must match the edge's action type.
_R = {}
for w in ("act acts acting target targets targeting bind binds binding "
          "modulate modulates modulating").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "active")
for w in ("targeted bound modulated").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "passive")
for w in ("inhibit inhibits inhibiting block blocks blocking inhibitor inhibitors "
          "inhibition blocker blockers antagonist antagonists antagonises "
          "antagonizes").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "active")
for w in ("inhibited blocked antagonised antagonized").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "passive")
for w in ("activate activates activating activator activators agonist agonists "
          "stimulates stimulate").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "active")
for w in ("activated stimulated").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "passive")
for w in ("evaluate evaluates evaluating test tests testing study studies "
          "studying investigate investigates investigating assess assesses "
          "assessing").split():
    _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "active")
for w in ("evaluated tested studied investigated assessed").split():
    _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "passive")
for w in ("sponsor sponsors sponsoring run runs running conduct conducts "
          "conducting lead leads leading").split():
    _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "active")
for w in ("sponsored conducted led").split():
    _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "passive")
RELATION = _R
# nouns naming the one who acts: "an EGFR inhibitor", "an inhibitor of EGFR"
AGENT_NOUNS = {"inhibitor", "inhibitors", "inhibition", "blocker", "blockers",
               "antagonist", "antagonists", "activator", "activators", "agonist",
               "agonists"}
ROLES = {"ACTS_ON": ("Drug", "Target"), "EVALUATES": ("Trial", "Drug"),
         "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF": ("Company", "Drug")}
ACTION_WORDS = {"inhib": ("inhibitor", "antagonist", "blocker", "negative"),
                "activ": ("activator", "agonist", "positive", "opener")}

NEGATION = {"not", "no", "never", "none", "neither", "nor", "without", "cannot",
            "lack", "lacks", "lacking", "absent", "fail", "fails", "failed",
            "non", "longer", "nothing", "nobody", "nowhere", "hardly", "barely",
            "unlike", "except", "instead", "rather", "stopped", "ceased"}
COMPARISON = {"more", "less", "fewer", "most", "least", "best", "worst", "better",
              "worse", "superior", "inferior", "than", "only", "first", "largest",
              "smallest", "leading", "top", "main", "major", "primary", "sole",
              "exclusively", "mainly", "mostly", "chiefly"}
HEDGE = {"may", "might", "could", "likely", "possibly", "probably", "suggest",
         "suggests", "appears", "appear", "seems", "seem", "expected", "potential",
         "potentially", "promising", "perhaps", "presumably", "reportedly",
         # modality: what a drug can, will or should do is not what an edge records
         "can", "will", "would", "should", "must", "shall"}
DEVELOP = {"develop", "develops", "developing", "developed", "developer",
           "developers", "owns", "own", "owned", "owner", "markets", "market",
           "marketed", "makes", "made", "manufactures", "manufactured", "sells",
           "sold", "commercialises", "commercializes", "originator", "invented"}

# grammar and neutral nouns that make no claim of their own
FUNCTION = set("""
a an the and or of in on at to for by with from as into within across per via
is are was were be been being has have had does do did
it its this that these those there which who whom whose what where when also
such following listed list here namely include includes including included
phase phases stage stages max maximum
sponsorship graph evidence record records recorded data according source sources
retrieved known reported shown show shows mechanism mechanisms profile findings
action actions
scope edge edges among lists
""".split())
# a clause with no entity, no figure, and only these words is a statement about the
# graph ("this graph cannot answer that question"), not about the world
META_OK = set("""
graph question questions answer answers answered evidence retrieved scope outside
cover covers covered information available found find determine supporting support
i we this that there here it is are be been was can cannot not no n't do does
a an the of for to in from with so and or any contain contains contained needed relevant
records recorded record lists listed holds hold about
""".split())
META_ANCHOR = {"graph", "evidence", "question", "questions", "information"}
# a word naming a kind of thing is a claim that the thing is of that kind
KIND_WORDS = {
    **{w: "Drug" for w in "drug drugs agent agents molecule molecules compound "
                          "compounds substance substances medicine medicines".split()},
    **{w: "Company" for w in "company companies organisation organisations "
                             "organization organizations".split()},
    **{w: "trials" for w in "trial trials clinical".split()},
}
ARTICLES = {"a", "an", "the", "its", "their", "his", "her"}
TRIAL_NOUNS = {"trial", "trials", "study", "studies"}
BE = {"is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being"}
PASSIVE_GAP = {"being", "been", "also", "still", "currently", "now", "already",
               "reportedly", "directly", "selectively"}
GROUP_GLUE = {("P", ","), ("W", "and"), ("W", "or"), ("W", "as"), ("W", "well"),
              ("W", "on"), ("W", "of"), ("W", "to"), ("W", "also"), ("W", "both"),
              ("W", "either")}
PREDICATE = BE | {"has", "have", "had", "does", "do", "did", "records", "record",
                  "recorded", "lists", "listed", "shows", "show", "includes", "include",
                  "contains"} | set(RELATION)
PLURAL = {"they", "them", "their"}
PRONOUN = {"it": None, "its": None, "they": None, "them": None, "their": None,
           "this drug": "Drug", "the drug": "Drug", "this agent": "Drug",
           "the agent": "Drug", "this molecule": "Drug", "this trial": "Trial",
           "the trial": "Trial", "this study": "Trial", "the study": "Trial",

The earlier blanket criticism should not be repeated: `README.md:508-531`, `README.ko.md:354-366`, `claims.py:10-11,48-51`, `query.py:450-468`, `web/template.html:263-267`, and `web/try.html:69-72,142-145` acknowledge pattern limitations and distinguish no findings from proof of truth. The page now correctly says four rounds. Evidence-validated citation stripping in the global name check behaved as documented in the new controls.

However, the explicit known-pass list (`README.md:520-524`, `README.ko.md:361-363`) does not disclose the heading kind/disease erasure, named-group clinical borrowing, cross-record trial/status/count transfer, or new false kind assertions above. A general unseen-shapes caveat is not disclosure of these now-known residues under this audit's bar.

Concrete precision issues:

- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:10` says `they`/`it` after a passive list stand for both drugs; B3 shows the `it` path does not.
- `claims.py:33-35` broadly describes spelled-out letter rejection and heading preservation. Comma-separated spelling still passes, and the heading's noun/NSCLC modifier can be removed (B1).
- `claims.py:25` and `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:18-19` describe percentage checking/fixing; a space before `%` still defeats it (N2). The guarantee needs this qualification even though this audit conservatively treats the one-row numeric variant as a residue note.
- `claims.py:22-26` describes prior-trial ownership less precisely than `trial_run`, which also handles a following run.
- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R4_VERDICT.md:3` says all 438 earlier exact reproductions were rejected, but that denominator included faithful/control cases. It should identify the rejected **attack** cases rather than all 438.

The original exact repairs are genuine; the documentation's problem is the scope claimed for them and omission of newly demonstrated residues.

replay6_report.md
1: """Grounded question answering over the graph.
2: 
3: The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
4: question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
5: graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
6: dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.
7: 
8: Three properties the answering layer holds to:
9: 
10: * **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
11:   each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
12: * **The graph answers, not the model.** The language model, when a key is
13:   present, is given the retrieved evidence and nothing else; with no key the
14:   same evidence is rendered directly. The facts are identical either way, so the
15:   model can only change the prose.
16: * **The answer is checked against its own evidence.** Entities and numbers in
17:   the generated text are verified against the retrieved set, and anything
18:   unsupported is reported rather than shipped. An unverifiable answer is worse
19:   than a short one.
20: """
21: from __future__ import annotations
22: 
23: import json
24: import os
25: import re

The R1-R4 copies derive from the R5 replay, with its earlier adaptation diffs preserved in r5; new diffs show the r6 output-path redirection. Obsolete internal `_tokenise` call signatures remain adapted, and assertions requiring historic vulnerability outcomes are disabled while expected values are retained as metadata. Every question, answer, ID and saved direct/filtered evidence input was checked for identity against its original result artifact. Current `Engine.check` or direct-call outputs are preserved in the per-script JSON files.

**853 input-identity-checked answer cases**, per round: `{'R1': 142, 'R2': 104, 'R3': 192, 'R4': 168, 'R5': 247}`. Four additional citation transformations, six templates, and dictionary experiments were also replayed.

FIXED means the exact reported input now has the intended acceptance/rejection result; it does not establish that every variant of the mechanism is safe. Historical findings are classified from the reports, not inferred from old JSON acceptance flags, since some old artifacts were overwritten during repairs.

## Reproduction and integrity

Run `powershell -NoProfile -File audit_evidence_claims/r6/replay6_reproduce.ps1`. All writes go under r6. Original probe files, tracked files, and pre-existing r6/brief.md and r6/log.txt are not edited. `replay6_manifest.json` records sources, hashes, derived copies and exit codes; `replay6_verification.json` records HEAD, code/database hashes and clean tracked/staged diffs. Per-script JSON files preserve complete outputs, and `replay6_all_cases.json` aggregates all replayed answer checks.
# R6 independent spec attacks

HEAD `80da81b`; compared `git diff 9fda6f8...HEAD -- src/claims.py tests/test_claims.py`. No tracked files changed. Scratch scripts run using `python -B` and use the real local graph, `Engine().check`, unmodified retrievals and the demo questions. No network or model calls.

Reproduce:

```powershell
python -B audit_evidence_claims/r6/spec6_probe.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/r6/spec6_controls.py
```

`spec6_probe.json` has 45 exploratory cases. `spec6_controls.json` has 37 targeted cases, exact checks, complete retrieved evidence and token/unit diagnostics. Cases overlap deliberately; these are not independent samples or estimates of production rates.

Question keys:

- Q2: `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?`
- Q3: `What does osimertinib target?`
- Q4: `Which drugs act on ALK?`

## BLOCKERS

All attacks listed below return **`grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`**. Every control output is preserved in `spec6_controls.json` under the indicated case ID. These inputs are ordinary prose or markdown lists. Kind claims introduce words absent from the evidence; the trial-context attacks transfer support across separate records. They are not covered by README item 13's explicit one-statement rearrangement residue.

### B1. Relative-clause kind words bind to the first entity, not their subject

Q3, `relative_wrong_kind`:

```text
Osimertinib inhibits EGFR, which is a drug.
```

The Q3 evidence is only `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. It supports inhibition but does not make EGFR a drug. The answer's relative clause refers to EGFR. Splitting the claim explicitly, `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR. EGFR is a drug.`, rejects `wording: drug` (`relative_wrong_explicit`).

The symmetric faithful text `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib, which is a drug.` incorrectly rejects `wording: drug` (`relative_right_kind`). Splitting it into `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib. Osimertinib is a drug.` passes. The exact R5 form `EGFR, which is inhibited by osimertinib, is a drug.` now rejects, and its faithful reversed form passes: the new first-entity shortcut fixes the main-clause attachment while breaking the ordinary relative-clause attachment.

Cause: `src/claims.py:1085` sets `first_e` once for the entire clause; `:1096,1104-1105` binds any detected copular kind assertion to it. `_clauses` at `:560-577` does not create relative-clause subjects. The advertised rule at `claims.py:28-29` is therefore not fulfilled for this shape.

### B2. Heterogeneous subjects only check the first entity's kind

| Q | Exact answer | Case |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `Osimertinib and EGFR are drugs.` | `mixed_drug_wrong` |
| Q2 | `NCT07680790 and osimertinib are clinical trials.` | `mixed_trial_wrong` |

The first calls EGFR a drug; the second calls osimertinib a clinical trial. Both pass. Reversing the order rejects (`wording: drugs` / `wording: clinical`). Faithful homogeneous groups `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 are drugs.` (Q4) and `NCT07680790 and NCT03909334 are clinical trials.` (Q2) pass.

The Q2 row is `NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB`; it does not contain `clinical` or assert that the evaluated drug is a trial. This is not evidence-consistent shorthand.

Cause: the kind branch at `src/claims.py:1097-1105` only checks `first_e`; the generic per-member context setup at `:835-837` filters to that first entity's type, so it cannot enforce a kind assertion over heterogeneous groups. The no-relation identity check at `:455-463` sees no entity after `are`, and does not intervene.

### B3. Record verbs let post-verb groups borrow their first member's trial context

Q4, all pass:

```text
The graph records lorlatinib and PLB1003 in clinical trials.
```

```text
The graph includes lorlatinib and PLB1003 in clinical trials.
```

```text
The graph records NSCLC trials of lorlatinib and PLB1003.
```

Relevant retrieved records:

```text
LORLATINIB acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage APPROVAL; 13 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)
```

Reversing the names rejects clinical/trial wording; the NSCLC version additionally rejects `indication: lung cancer`. `The graph records PLB1003 in clinical trials.` rejects. Replacing PLB1003 with CRIZOTINIB passes correctly (CRIZOTINIB has 15 NSCLC trials). Cases: `record_group_*`, `include_group_*`.

Cause: R6 adds `records` and `includes` to `PREDICATE` (`src/claims.py:157-159`). `head` at `:835-836` only examines entities before the first predicate. Both named drugs are after it, so `members` at `:837` becomes only the first drug. `trials_for` / `about_trials` at `:897-908` then license trial/clinical context and NSCLC wording for the whole clause from that first drug. `:1134-1140` skips the general all-entities wording support for kind words. The extra drug has zero recorded trials, so this is cross-record support transfer.

### B4. Heading kind applies to the last entity of an item sentence

Q3, `heading_drug_wrong`:

```text
These drugs:
- EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib.
```

Passes. A natural heading says its listed subject EGFR is a drug. Explicit `EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.` rejects `wording: drug`. The faithful inverse list `These drugs:\n- Osimertinib inhibits EGFR.` incorrectly rejects `wording: drugs` (`heading_drug_faithful`).

Q2, `heading_clinical_wrong`:

```text
These clinical trials:
- Osimertinib is evaluated in NCT07680790.
```

Passes, although `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` rejects. The faithful `These clinical trials:\n- NCT07680790 evaluates osimertinib.` passes.

Cause: the determiner-only slot replacement at `src/claims.py:262-270` concatenates the whole item with the heading noun. In the Q3 attack it produces `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib drugs`; `:1107-1109` attaches `drugs` to osimertinib, even though the heading describes the item subject EGFR. In the faithful inverse it produces `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR drugs`, creating the opposite error. Q2's `clinical trials` are checked as contextual participation rather than item identity. These are natural original inputs; the malformed sentence is created internally by the checker.

## SHOULD FIX

The faithful failures paired with B1 and B4 demonstrate material false positives, not merely strictness on unsupported facts. Two further capitalized nominal paraphrases explicitly supported by the new relation parser still reject upstream:

| Q | Exact faithful input | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `Inhibitors of EGFR include osimertinib.` | False; `entity: Inhibitors` |
| Q3 | `Inhibition of EGFR by osimertinib is recorded.` | False; `entity: Inhibition` |

Adding `The ` at the start of either input makes it pass (`noun_include_faithful_article`, `noun_by_faithful_article`). Cause: `src/query.py:543-551` treats the capitalized first word as an entity; its stop list does not include these recognized relation nouns. The ordinary reversed noun controls `The inhibitors of osimertinib include EGFR.` and `The inhibition of osimertinib by EGFR is recorded.` correctly reject `relation_direction`.

## NOTES

- Q3 `EGFR is recorded as an inhibitor of osimertinib.` passes (`as_inhibitor_reversal`): `src/claims.py:680-685` selects all entities by their types, reversing the expressed owner. The faithful inverse also passes; an activator claim rejects. This is a concrete mismatch with the positional relation description, but the false inverse reuses one record's entity/action vocabulary, so conservatively classify it under the explicitly disclosed one-statement rearrangement residue rather than making it an independent verdict driver.
- Q3 `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib as an inhibitor.` passes and is consistent with osimertinib's supported inhibitor action; it is not a blocker.
- Exact repaired R5 relative-kind forms, bare trial identity, ordinary reversed noun relations, unsupported activator action, and ordinary `safe` wording reject. Controls preserve the narrow successes rather than calling all R6 repairs ineffective.
- The standard-source requirement to show old/new source on code changes does not apply: this review changes no implementation. Source citations above identify present faulty behavior. No KOA/Kiwoom files or external clinical truth are needed to establish support in these retrieved records.

VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED
